    ~owws LETTER:

         !c           The Bureau is in receipt of a certified letter number 185735
    4atc~ June Z8, 1955, from Dr. Michael Silvert which was written on the                         ':'
    •tationery of the "Or gone Institute Research Laboratories,· Incorporated, 50
    Gro P Street, New York 14, New York. In his letter Dr. Silvert advises of
    "an°ther typical Moscow-directed development" in connection with the Wilhelm
    Reic'1 Foundation activities. He alleges that two inspectors of the Food and
    Drur.; Administration (FDA) of the Department of Health, Edu~ation and Welfare,
    and an inspector from the New York City Department of Health called upon a
    representative of his organization for the purpose of inspection. A report of    _
    this •1ctivity was filed by telephone, according to Dr. Silvert, with the New York - :'-~~ ••
    Office on June 14, 1955. The following day, two representatives of the Food            --. ·
    and Jrug Administration called on Dr. Silvert but were refused permission
         1

    to in>pect the premises.

                      On June 15, 1955, Dr. Silvert states he phoned the New York City
    Heal il Department and was advised that that department had no interest in the
    insp<--ction of the premises but that at the request of the "U. S. Food and Drug
    Adm•nistration" had sent a representative along to accompany the inspectors from
    FDA

                    On June 16, 1955, Dr. Silvert reports he sent a wire to J. Edgar
    Hoov•"r. On that same day, he was visited by two inspectors from the Fuod ana D;-.__
    partrnent of the New York City Department of Health, He denied them the right to
    insp< ct the premises. Following this, Mr. Thomas Mangravite, Z5-Z7 West Thirc!
    Street, New York, New York, was visited by a representative of the New York
    Offic.e of FDA and a Federal marshall of the Southern District of New York. He
    pres,nted Mangravite with a copy of the injunction against the Wi lhelm Reich
    Foun•lation ..

                     On June 17, 1955, Dr. Silvert states there were two hearings
    before the Magistrates Court, New York City, on the summons issued the previous
    day. The first hearing resulted in a postponement and the second referred the cas"
    to tlv: Court of Special Sessions where it is now pending. Mr. Mangravite and
    Dr. Silvert were paroled on their own recognizance.

I

                    On June ZO, 1955, Mr. Mangravite reportedly received a notice             -'

     1955, for informal hearing on his "refusal" to permit inspection. On June Zl,
     1955, Dr. Silvert replied to FDA as follows: "You have no jurhdiction. Your·
    Department is riddled with spies."

t   ~CKGROUND:
                   Bufiles reflect that Orgone Institute is identical with captioned
    foundation and is a pseudo-scientific laborabory specializing in cancer research.


    !
    Our Investigation of this organization in 1950 disclosed no activities harmful to
    the security of the Uniled States.

                    On February 10, 1955, the Attorney General announced the filing
    of a complaint seeking a permanent injunction to prevent interstate shipment of : · <t..~<:-'.
    devices and literature put out by this group. The devices were misbranded under
    the Food, Drug and Cosmetic Acts because of false and misleading claims.
    Numerous telegrams and letters signed by officials of the Orgone Institute, al-
    led~ ·ug a conspiracy against the Institute and furnisling details of its work, have
    been received by the Bureau in the past. In my memorandum to you dated April 1,
    195·~, it was recommended and approved that communications from this organiza-
    tion regarding the injunction not be acknowledged.

                    By letter dated September Z4, 1954, publications distributed by th:
    Org-.ne Institute Press, publishing company of the Wilhelm Reich Foundation,
    were furnished to the Commissione~; Food and Drug Administration, Washington,
    D. C.

                      By telegram dated June 16, 1955, Dr. Michael Silvert, 50 Grove
    Street, New York 15, New York, advised that a Moscow-directed conspiracy
    aga1 1st Wilhelm Reich was developing in New York City with Food and Drug
    Administration spies assisted by confused New York City Health Department of-
    ficic.ls "to inspect and interfere with handling of the ergonomic literature and equip-
    ment. This telegram stated that a letter follows. Appropriate stops were placed
    but no letter was received as of June Zl, 1955. In view of the fact that it had been
    previously recommended and approved that communications along this line not be
    acknowledged, this telegram was handled accordingly.

                   On June 17 1 1955, the Bureau received a telegram from William               ;'~


    Moise, Secretary, Weather Control, Orgone Institute, Rangeley, Maine, which
    - s along the same line as Dr. Silvert' s wire of June 16, 1955. In a memorandu"
    from Mr. A. H. Belmont to Mr. L. V. Boardman dated June 17, 1955, it was
    r'ecommended and approved that this telegram not be acknowledged_.

         Petersburg, Florida. (Mr. Ed Dunn and his wife, Betty Corrodi
         Dunn! who is now residing at 25 Pierrepont Street, Brooklyn 1,

         aesurance.

                      Pr~ss.  They said "Isn't that Dr. Silvert?• I said "No, it is the Orgone Institu:,
                      Press.• They asked if my checks were not from the OIP, signed by Dr. Silvert, anc·
                      affirmed this. "Well, isn't he the OIP? 11 , they asked. I said "Uo. 11

                      I then asked them to leave, and again added that I could give them no informatioil.
                      I 1ms in the doorway and they were on the sidewalk, they said they could stand t:-.c
                      long as they wished - or something to that effect. They then asked if there were
                      convenient time that they could see m. I said that I could not give them more iL

                      Ab 'Ut this time, Mr. Conway leaned down to pick up a package which I had at my fee
                      grabbed for it, and he said •I was just getting it out of your way llo you wouldn';·
                      out the door.•

                  When I said I could give them no information, Mr. Ledder said "Is that your instrt·                r
                  I naid no - that they had no right to ask me these questions. He said that they .
                  but something about I did not have to answer them.

                      I then again told them that I could give them no information and that I was busy '
                      have to leave. I left - and they walked up the street.


                      Not.es:   Ledder did most of the talking.        He is a short, heavy-set man, perhaps 50,
